Hi. Um, well, I came in because I’ve barely been sleeping at all since this warehouse inspection I had to do. I'm an insurance underwriter, so normally I'm just at my desk looking at risk models and spreadsheets. I actually really like the desk work, it's quiet, you know? I have this great little setup by the window with all my plants. But they sent me out to this site... and now I just lie in bed every night replaying bits of it over and over.

Yeah, actually, I do. It's really weird. I'll just be at my desk, um, reviewing a standard commercial policy or looking at a loss report. Which, by the way, loss reports are usually incredibly dry to read. My coworker Greg always formats his with this tiny font—Arial size 9, I think? I've told him a million times it's terrible for eye strain, especially since we stare at monitors all day. I actually ended up buying those blue-light glasses just because of him. I got these really cute tortoiseshell frames online. 

But anyway, I'll just be looking at the screen, trying to read Greg's tiny font, and out of nowhere, I get hit with this... this really sharp image. Like, I suddenly just see the worker lying on the floor again, and the soot in the air. It’s like my body just completely reacts before I can even stop it, like I tense up and panic. 

And ugh, the soot was just everywhere that day. It completely ruined my favorite beige blazer. I took it to the dry cleaner on 4th street—the one next to that bakery that makes those amazing almond croissants? But they said the stain was totally set. I was so upset because I got it on clearance at Nordstrom and you can never find those deals twice. But, um, yeah, to answer your question, it definitely happens while I'm awake too.

Um, yeah, I guess I have been. Like, I haven’t even touched the actual file for that warehouse yet. I just keep pushing it to the corner of my desk. I keep meaning to re-record it, but I just never get around to it. 

But anyway, yeah, I just can't deal with those industrial accounts right now. Honestly, even just seeing an email about one of them pop up in my inbox makes my stomach completely drop, so I just avoid opening them altogether.

Um, no, not really. I don't think I've stopped doing things I enjoy because they remind me of the warehouse. Like, my hobbies don't really have anything to do with work anyway. 

Like, I love to bake. I actually just started getting into making sourdough bread, which is a whole process, let me tell you. The chef there was so funny, he kept yelling at us in Italian when we rolled the dough too thin. It was such a fun night. We ended up buying a pasta machine for the house, but honestly, it's such a pain to clean that we've only used it like twice. It just sits on the top shelf of the pantry collecting dust next to the juicer I bought during my health kick phase in January. 

But um, no, my hobbies and the things I like to do don't really remind me of the warehouse at all, so I haven't really been avoiding them for that reason.

Um, they're incredibly upsetting. Like, I'll just be sitting there during the day, reviewing a standard policy or looking at a loss report, and suddenly I get hit with this sharp, vivid image of the worker on the floor and all the soot in the air. I had to stand up there in front of like, twenty people, and my hands were shaking so bad I could barely hold my laser pointer. I bought this really nice blazer for it, though. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no, not really. I haven't really avoided seeing my friends or anything because of the memories. Honestly, I've still been trying to go out when I can. 

Like last weekend, I went to this brunch place with my friend Jessica. Have you ever been to The Rusty Spoon? It's that new place over on 4th Street. They have these incredible mimosa flights where you get to try four different flavors. We had peach, mango, strawberry, and this weird lavender one that actually tasted kind of like soap, to be honest. I don't get the whole lavender flavor trend. People are putting it in lattes and lemonade now, and it just feels like I'm drinking a candle, you know? Anyway, Jessica was telling me all about her new puppy. She got a goldendoodle, and apparently, it's chewing up all her baseboards. I told her she needs to get that bitter apple spray, but she said she tried it and the dog actually likes the taste. Dogs are so weird like that. I had a hamster when I was a kid named Mr. Whiskers, and he used to chew on the metal bars of his cage all night long. It drove me crazy, I had to move his cage into the hallway just so I could sleep. 

But um, no, I haven't been avoiding social stuff because of the warehouse thoughts. I'm still seeing people.

Um, no, not really. I mean, I was just there to observe the site for the policy renewal, so I didn't really have anything to do with the actual operations or the equipment. So no, I don't feel like it was my fault or anything. 

Though, speaking of the policy renewals, our office just switched to this new digital filing system for them and it is an absolute nightmare. My coworker Brenda—she's the one who sits right next to the breakroom—she accidentally deleted an entire client folder yesterday. The IT guy, Kevin, had to come all the way down from the fourth floor to fix it. Kevin's actually really nice, though. He always brings in donuts on Fridays. My boyfriend absolutely loves Hawaiian pizza, but I just can't do it. The texture of warm fruit is just so weird to me. Like, cooked apples in a pie? Totally fine. But warm pineapple with cheese? People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, talking about it? Well, I haven't really had to talk about it much, but I've definitely been avoiding anything to do with it at work. Like, I’ve been completely avoiding the actual warehouse file. And if I get calls about any of our industrial accounts, I just let them go straight to voicemail. Even just seeing those emails in my inbox makes my stomach completely drop. 

Which is actually super annoying because my inbox is already a total disaster. We have this mandatory archiving rule where anything older than ninety days is supposed to get moved to a subfolder, but the rule never works right. I spent like three hours last Tuesday just manually dragging emails over. I was listening to this true crime podcast while I did it—have you heard of "Murder in the Midwest"? It's so good. The host has this really soothing voice, which is kind of weird considering she's talking about unsolved homicides. I started listening to podcasts because my commute is awful now. They've been doing construction on I-95 for what feels like three years. Every time they say they're going to finish a lane, they just block off another one. Last week, I was stuck behind this truck carrying what looked like giant wind turbine blades. Have you ever seen those up close? They are terrifyingly huge. It makes you wonder how they even turn corners without taking out a traffic light or something. My dad used to drive a truck, actually. Mostly just regional stuff, delivering furniture. He had this old CB radio in the cab and he taught me all the trucker slang when I was little. "Breaker breaker one nine," you know? 

But yeah, I just can't deal with the warehouse stuff right now. I just ignore the calls and emails.

I love tapas because you get to try a little bit of everything without committing to one big meal. \n\nBut, um, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, the sleep stuff and the flashbacks... it's been going on for about a week now, ever since the warehouse thing.\n\nDoctor: How vivid or real do these flashbacks feel when they happen to you?\n\nPatient: Um, they feel incredibly real. Like, terrifyingly real. During the day, I’ll just be sitting at my desk, reviewing a standard policy or looking at a loss report, and suddenly I just get hit with this sharp, vivid image of the worker on the floor and all the soot in the air. And it feels like my body reacts before I can even stop it, like my heart starts racing and I can almost smell the smoke again. \n\nWhich is awful because the air quality in my office is already so bad. I also bought an electric kettle for my desk that day because the coffee in the breakroom is just undrinkable. It's always burnt and tastes like pennies. I've started bringing my own loose-leaf chamomile tea, which is supposed to be really calming, but honestly, it hasn't been helping much lately. \n\nBut yeah, when those images hit me, it feels like I'm right back there in the warehouse for a second. We have this new performance tracking software they just rolled out, and it logs every single call we miss and how long it takes us to respond to emails. It's called, like, OmniTrack or something? The interface is just terrible. It's all these bright neon colors that give me a headache, and the dashboard is so cluttered. My friend Sarah, who sits in the cubicle next to me, she actually submitted a complaint to IT about it because she said the font is too small to read. Sarah's great, she always brings in these amazing homemade blueberry muffins on Fridays. Her husband is a baker, so they always have so many baked goods at their house. I don't know how she stays so thin, honestly. She runs marathons, though, so I guess that helps. I tried getting into running once, but I bought the wrong kind of shoes and ended up with awful blisters.\n\nBut um, yeah, I've just been avoiding anything to do with the warehouse or industrial stuff entirely. I just can't deal with it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided doing things you normally enjoy because they remind you of the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think I've stopped doing things I enjoy because they remind me of the warehouse. Like, my hobbies don't really have anything to do with work anyway. \n\nLike, I love to bake. I actually just started getting into making sourdough bread, which is a whole process, let me tell you. You have to feed the starter every single day, and it smells kind of like beer? My boyfriend hates the smell, he says it makes the whole kitchen smell like a brewery. He's more of a savory guy anyway, he loves when I make homemade pasta. We actually took a pasta-making class for our anniversary last year at this little Italian place downtown. It just sits on the top shelf of the pantry collecting dust next to the juicer I bought during my health kick phase in January. \n\nBut um, no, my hobbies and the things I like to do don't really remind me of the warehouse at all, so I haven't really been avoiding them for that reason.\n\nDoctor: How upsetting or disturbing are these flashbacks and memories of the warehouse inspection to you?\n\nPatient: Um, they're incredibly upsetting. Like, I'll just be sitting there during the day, reviewing a standard policy or looking at a loss report, and suddenly I get hit with this sharp, vivid image of the worker on the floor and all the soot in the air. It's so disturbing, and it feels like my body just reacts before I can even stop it. My heart races, my chest gets tight... \n\nWhich is actually really similar to how I felt when I had to give that big presentation to the regional managers last month. I am terrified of public speaking. I had to stand up there in front of like, twenty people, and my hands were shaking so bad I could barely hold my laser pointer. I bought this really nice blazer for it, though. It's navy blue with these little gold buttons, I found it at Nordstrom Rack for like sixty percent off. I love Nordstrom Rack, you can find such good deals if you're willing to dig through the clearance racks. My mom taught me how to bargain hunt when I was a kid. We used to go to all the garage sales in our neighborhood on Saturday mornings. She'd always haggle with people over, like, a fifty-cent vase. It used to embarrass me so much, but now I totally get it. \n\nBut yeah, the memories are just really disturbing. It's like I have no control over my body when they happen.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided social activities because of the memories or thoughts related to the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. We had peach, mango, strawberry, and this weird lavender one that actually tasted kind of like soap, to be honest. I don't get the whole lavender flavor trend. People are putting it in lattes and lemonade now, and it just feels like I'm drinking a candle, you know? Anyway, Jessica was telling me all about her new puppy. She got a goldendoodle, and apparently, it's chewing up all her baseboards. I told her she needs to get that bitter apple spray, but she said she tried it and the dog actually likes the taste. Dogs are so weird like that. I had a hamster when I was a kid named Mr. Whiskers, and he used to chew on the metal bars of his cage all night long. It drove me crazy, I had to move his cage into the hallway just so I could sleep. \n\nBut um, no, I haven't been avoiding social stuff because of the warehouse thoughts. I'm still seeing people.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible in any way for what happened during the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I was just there to observe the site for the policy renewal, so I didn't really have anything to do with the actual operations or the equipment. So no, I don't feel like it was my fault or anything. \n\nThough, speaking of the policy renewals, our office just switched to this new digital filing system for them and it is an absolute nightmare. My coworker Brenda—she's the one who sits right next to the breakroom—she accidentally deleted an entire client folder yesterday. The IT guy, Kevin, had to come all the way down from the fourth floor to fix it. Kevin's actually really nice, though. He always brings in donuts on Fridays. Last week he brought these maple bacon ones from that little bakery on 5th Street. I usually don't like bacon on sweet things, you know? It's kind of like when people put pineapple on pizza. My boyfriend absolutely loves Hawaiian pizza, but I just can't do it. The texture of warm fruit is just so weird to me. Like, cooked apples in a pie? Totally fine. But warm pineapple with cheese? No thank you. We actually got into a huge debate about it last time we ordered takeout, and we ended up just getting Thai food instead. Um, going back there? No, I definitely haven't gone back. There's really no reason for me to, since I only went out there for the policy renewal observation anyway. I mean, usually, I don't even do site visits. I'm an underwriter, so I spend like 90% of my day just staring at spreadsheets and risk assessment models. 

Actually, speaking of spreadsheets, they just updated Excel on our work computers and they moved all the buttons around on the ribbon. It is driving me absolutely insane. Like, why do they have to change things that are already working perfectly fine? It's like when grocery stores remodel and suddenly the peanut butter is in the baking aisle instead of with the jelly. I spent twenty minutes looking for it at Trader Joe's last Sunday. I ended up buying this cookie butter stuff instead because I gave up. Have you tried that? It's literally just ground-up cookies in a jar. It's amazing but also incredibly dangerous to have in the house. I ate half the jar with a spoon while I was watching that new baking competition show on Netflix. The one where they have to make cakes that look like everyday objects? It's wild. Someone made a cake that looked exactly like a pair of Crocs. I don't even understand how you get fondant to look like rubber. I tried to make a birthday cake for my sister once, just a regular round cake, and it completely collapsed in the middle. I had to fill the crater with extra frosting and just tell her it was a "frosting surprise" cake. She was nice about it, but it looked terrible. 

But um, yeah, going back to the warehouse? No, I haven't been back. I just stay in my cubicle.

We're supposed to go out to dinner this weekend to celebrate, to this new tapas place that just opened up near the river. I love tapas because you get to try a little bit of everything without committing to one big meal. \n\nBut, um, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, the sleep stuff and the flashbacks... it's been going on for about a week now, ever since the warehouse thing.\n\nDoctor: How vivid or real do these flashbacks feel when they happen to you?\n\nPatient: Um, they feel incredibly real. Like, terrifyingly real. During the day, I’ll just be sitting at my desk, reviewing a standard policy or looking at a loss report, and suddenly I just get hit with this sharp, vivid image of the worker on the floor and all the soot in the air. And it feels like my body reacts before I can even stop it, like my heart starts racing and I can almost smell the smoke again. \n\nWhich is awful because the air quality in my office is already so bad. The building is super old, like built in the seventies, and I swear they haven't updated the HVAC system since then. I actually bought this little personal air purifier for my desk? It's shaped like a little white egg and it plugs right into my USB port. I got it on Amazon Prime Day last year when they had all those crazy deals. I also bought an electric kettle for my desk that day because the coffee in the breakroom is just undrinkable. It's always burnt and tastes like pennies. I've started bringing my own loose-leaf chamomile tea, which is supposed to be really calming, but honestly, it hasn't been helping much lately. \n\nBut yeah, when those images hit me, it feels like I'm right back there in the warehouse for a second. It's really intense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself avoiding thoughts or feelings related to the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I definitely do. I mean, I've been completely avoiding the warehouse file at work. I just... I can't even look at it. And any calls that come in about industrial accounts? I just let them go straight to voicemail because even the thought of opening those emails or talking about it makes my stomach completely drop. \n\nWhich is actually really bad for my metrics this quarter. We have this new performance tracking software they just rolled out, and it logs every single call we miss and how long it takes us to respond to emails. It's called, like, OmniTrack or something? The interface is just terrible. It's all these bright neon colors that give me a headache, and the dashboard is so cluttered. My friend Sarah, who sits in the cubicle next to me, she actually submitted a complaint to IT about it because she said the font is too small to read. Sarah's great, she always brings in these amazing homemade blueberry muffins on Fridays. Her husband is a baker, so they always have so many baked goods at their house. I don't know how she stays so thin, honestly. She runs marathons, though, so I guess that helps. I tried getting into running once, but I bought the wrong kind of shoes and ended up with awful blisters.\n\nBut um, yeah, I've just been avoiding anything to do with the warehouse or industrial stuff entirely. I just can't deal with it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided doing things you normally enjoy because they remind you of the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I don't think I've stopped doing things I enjoy because they remind me of the warehouse. Like, my hobbies don't really have anything to do with work anyway. \n\nLike, I love to bake. I actually just started getting into making sourdough bread, which is a whole process, let me tell you. You have to feed the starter every single day, and it smells kind of like beer? My boyfriend hates the smell, he says it makes the whole kitchen smell like a brewery. He's more of a savory guy anyway, he loves when I make homemade pasta. We actually took a pasta-making class for our anniversary last year at this little Italian place downtown. The chef there was so funny, he kept yelling at us in Italian when we rolled the dough too thin. It was such a fun night. We ended up buying a pasta machine for the house, but honestly, it's such a pain to clean that we've only used it like twice. It just sits on the top shelf of the pantry collecting dust next to the juicer I bought during my health kick phase in January. \n\nBut um, no, my hobbies and the things I like to do don't really remind me of the warehouse at all, so I haven't really been avoiding them for that reason.\n\nDoctor: How upsetting or disturbing are these flashbacks and memories of the warehouse inspection to you?\n\nPatient: Um, they're incredibly upsetting. Like, I'll just be sitting there during the day, reviewing a standard policy or looking at a loss report, and suddenly I get hit with this sharp, vivid image of the worker on the floor and all the soot in the air. It's so disturbing, and it feels like my body just reacts before I can even stop it. My heart races, my chest gets tight... \n\nWhich is actually really similar to how I felt when I had to give that big presentation to the regional managers last month. I am terrified of public speaking. I had to stand up there in front of like, twenty people, and my hands were shaking so bad I could barely hold my laser pointer. I bought this really nice blazer for it, though. It's navy blue with these little gold buttons, I found it at Nordstrom Rack for like sixty percent off. I love Nordstrom Rack, you can find such good deals if you're willing to dig through the clearance racks. My mom taught me how to bargain hunt when I was a kid. We used to go to all the garage sales in our neighborhood on Saturday mornings. She'd always haggle with people over, like, a fifty-cent vase. It used to embarrass me so much, but now I totally get it. \n\nBut yeah, the memories are just really disturbing. It's like I have no control over my body when they happen.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided social activities because of the memories or thoughts related to the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I haven't really avoided seeing my friends or anything because of the memories. Honestly, I've still been trying to go out when I can. \n\nLike last weekend, I went to this brunch place with my friend Jessica. Have you ever been to The Rusty Spoon? It's that new place over on 4th Street. They have these incredible mimosa flights where you get to try four different flavors. We had peach, mango, strawberry, and this weird lavender one that actually tasted kind of like soap, to be honest. I don't get the whole lavender flavor trend. People are putting it in lattes and lemonade now, and it just feels like I'm drinking a candle, you know? Anyway, Jessica was telling me all about her new puppy. She got a goldendoodle, and apparently, it's chewing up all her baseboards. I told her she needs to get that bitter apple spray, but she said she tried it and the dog actually likes the taste. Dogs are so weird like that. I had a hamster when I was a kid named Mr. Whiskers, and he used to chew on the metal bars of his cage all night long. It drove me crazy, I had to move his cage into the hallway just so I could sleep. \n\nBut um, no, I haven't been avoiding social stuff because of the warehouse thoughts. I'm still seeing people.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible in any way for what happened during the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I was just there to observe the site for the policy renewal, so I didn't really have anything to do with the actual operations or the equipment. So no, I don't feel like it was my fault or anything. \n\nThough, speaking of the policy renewals, our office just switched to this new digital filing system for them and it is an absolute nightmare. My coworker Brenda—she's the one who sits right next to the breakroom—she accidentally deleted an entire client folder yesterday. The IT guy, Kevin, had to come all the way down from the fourth floor to fix it. Kevin's actually really nice, though. He always brings in donuts on Fridays. Last week he brought these maple bacon ones from that little bakery on 5th Street. I usually don't like bacon on sweet things, you know? It's kind of like when people put pineapple on pizza. My boyfriend absolutely loves Hawaiian pizza, but I just can't do it. The texture of warm fruit is just so weird to me. Like, cooked apples in a pie? Totally fine. But warm pineapple with cheese? No thank you. We actually got into a huge debate about it last time we ordered takeout, and we ended up just getting Thai food instead. Pad Thai is always a safe bet. \n\nBut um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, about feeling responsible. No, I definitely don't feel guilty about what happened. It was just a terrible accident.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided talking about the warehouse inspection with anyone?\n\nPatient: Um, talking about it? Well, I haven't really had to talk about it much, but I've definitely been avoiding anything to do with it at work. Like, I’ve been completely avoiding the actual warehouse file. And if I get calls about any of our industrial accounts, I just let them go straight to voicemail. Last week, I was stuck behind this truck carrying what looked like giant wind turbine blades. Have you ever seen those up close? They are terrifyingly huge. It makes you wonder how they even turn corners without taking out a traffic light or something. My dad used to drive a truck, actually. Mostly just regional stuff, delivering furniture. He had this old CB radio in the cab and he taught me all the trucker slang when I was little. \"Breaker breaker one nine,\" you know? \n\nBut yeah, I just can't deal with the warehouse stuff right now. I just ignore the calls and emails.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided going back to the place where the warehouse inspection happened?\n\nPatient: Um, going back there? No, I definitely haven't gone back. There's really no reason for me to, since I only went out there for the policy renewal observation anyway. I mean, usually, I don't even do site visits. I'm an underwriter, so I spend like 90% of my day just staring at spreadsheets and risk assessment models. \n\nActually, speaking of spreadsheets, they just updated Excel on our work computers and they moved all the buttons around on the ribbon. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, difficulties with my job? Yeah, definitely. I mean, I’ve missed deadlines on several underwriting reviews lately. And I've started handing off industrial account calls that I’m supposed to manage, which is really making me look unreliable. Especially since I just took on this role not that long ago. 

Which is so frustrating because I worked really hard to get this promotion. The interview process was intense. They had me do this mock presentation in front of the regional director, who is this super intimidating woman named Barbara. She wears these really sharp blazers and always has her hair in this perfect, tight bun. It kind of reminds me of my middle school principal, Mrs. Higgins. She used to stand in the hallway between classes with a stopwatch to make sure no one was lingering at their lockers. One time, I got detention just because my locker was jammed and I couldn't get my math textbook out fast enough. It was the locker right next to the water fountain, too, so it always smelled a little bit like mildew. I hated that school. They made us wear these awful maroon polo shirts that shrunk in the wash. My mom used to buy them two sizes too big so they'd last the whole year, but that just meant I spent the first semester looking like I was wearing a tent. I still can't wear the color maroon to this day. 

But um... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, my job. Yeah, I'm definitely struggling at work right now. Missing deadlines and handing off those calls. It's just... it's not good.

Um, startled? Yeah, oh my gosh, yes. It's actually getting kind of embarrassing. Like, every single time a door slams, or the printer jams, or even if someone just comes up behind my desk, I jump like something terrible is happening. Um, less interested? Didn't you... I feel like we just talked about this, right? Like with my pottery class? No, I still like doing all my normal stuff.

I also bought an electric kettle for my desk that day because the coffee in the breakroom is just undrinkable. It's always burnt and tastes like pennies. It drove me crazy, I had to move his cage into the hallway just so I could sleep. \n\nBut um, no, I haven't been avoiding social stuff because of the warehouse thoughts. I'm still seeing people.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or responsible in any way for what happened during the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I was just there to observe the site for the policy renewal, so I didn't really have anything to do with the actual operations or the equipment. So no, I don't feel like it was my fault or anything. \n\nThough, speaking of the policy renewals, our office just switched to this new digital filing system for them and it is an absolute nightmare. My coworker Brenda—she's the one who sits right next to the breakroom—she accidentally deleted an entire client folder yesterday. The IT guy, Kevin, had to come all the way down from the fourth floor to fix it. Kevin's actually really nice, though. He always brings in donuts on Fridays. Last week he brought these maple bacon ones from that little bakery on 5th Street. I usually don't like bacon on sweet things, you know? It's kind of like when people put pineapple on pizza. My boyfriend absolutely loves Hawaiian pizza, but I just can't do it. The texture of warm fruit is just so weird to me. Like, cooked apples in a pie? Totally fine. But warm pineapple with cheese? No thank you. We actually got into a huge debate about it last time we ordered takeout, and we ended up just getting Thai food instead. Pad Thai is always a safe bet. \n\nBut um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, about feeling responsible. No, I definitely don't feel guilty about what happened. It was just a terrible accident.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided talking about the warehouse inspection with anyone?\n\nPatient: Um, talking about it? Well, I haven't really had to talk about it much, but I've definitely been avoiding anything to do with it at work. Like, I’ve been completely avoiding the actual warehouse file. And if I get calls about any of our industrial accounts, I just let them go straight to voicemail. Even just seeing those emails in my inbox makes my stomach completely drop. \n\nWhich is actually super annoying because my inbox is already a total disaster. We have this mandatory archiving rule where anything older than ninety days is supposed to get moved to a subfolder, but the rule never works right. I spent like three hours last Tuesday just manually dragging emails over. I was listening to this true crime podcast while I did it—have you heard of \"Murder in the Midwest\"? It's so good. The host has this really soothing voice, which is kind of weird considering she's talking about unsolved homicides. I started listening to podcasts because my commute is awful now. They've been doing construction on I-95 for what feels like three years. Every time they say they're going to finish a lane, they just block off another one. Last week, I was stuck behind this truck carrying what looked like giant wind turbine blades. Have you ever seen those up close? They are terrifyingly huge. It makes you wonder how they even turn corners without taking out a traffic light or something. My dad used to drive a truck, actually. Mostly just regional stuff, delivering furniture. He had this old CB radio in the cab and he taught me all the trucker slang when I was little. \"Breaker breaker one nine,\" you know? \n\nBut yeah, I just can't deal with the warehouse stuff right now. I just ignore the calls and emails.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided going back to the place where the warehouse inspection happened?\n\nPatient: Um, going back there? No, I definitely haven't gone back. There's really no reason for me to, since I only went out there for the policy renewal observation anyway. I mean, usually, I don't even do site visits. I'm an underwriter, so I spend like 90% of my day just staring at spreadsheets and risk assessment models. \n\nActually, speaking of spreadsheets, they just updated Excel on our work computers and they moved all the buttons around on the ribbon. It is driving me absolutely insane. Like, why do they have to change things that are already working perfectly fine? It's like when grocery stores remodel and suddenly the peanut butter is in the baking aisle instead of with the jelly. I spent twenty minutes looking for it at Trader Joe's last Sunday. I ended up buying this cookie butter stuff instead because I gave up. Have you tried that? It's literally just ground-up cookies in a jar. It's amazing but also incredibly dangerous to have in the house. I ate half the jar with a spoon while I was watching that new baking competition show on Netflix. The one where they have to make cakes that look like everyday objects? It's wild. Someone made a cake that looked exactly like a pair of Crocs. I don't even understand how you get fondant to look like rubber. I tried to make a birthday cake for my sister once, just a regular round cake, and it completely collapsed in the middle. I had to fill the crater with extra frosting and just tell her it was a \"frosting surprise\" cake. She was nice about it, but it looked terrible. \n\nBut um, yeah, going back to the warehouse? No, I haven't been back. I just stay in my cubicle.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties in performing your job duties or maintaining relationships with others since the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um, difficulties with my job? Yeah, definitely. I mean, I’ve missed deadlines on several underwriting reviews lately. And I've started handing off industrial account calls that I’m supposed to manage, which is really making me look unreliable. Especially since I just took on this role not that long ago. \n\nWhich is so frustrating because I worked really hard to get this promotion. The interview process was intense. They had me do this mock presentation in front of the regional director, who is this super intimidating woman named Barbara. She wears these really sharp blazers and always has her hair in this perfect, tight bun. It kind of reminds me of my middle school principal, Mrs. Higgins. She used to stand in the hallway between classes with a stopwatch to make sure no one was lingering at their lockers. One time, I got detention just because my locker was jammed and I couldn't get my math textbook out fast enough. It was the locker right next to the water fountain, too, so it always smelled a little bit like mildew. I hated that school. They made us wear these awful maroon polo shirts that shrunk in the wash. My mom used to buy them two sizes too big so they'd last the whole year, but that just meant I spent the first semester looking like I was wearing a tent. I still can't wear the color maroon to this day. \n\nBut um... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, my job. Yeah, I'm definitely struggling at work right now. Missing deadlines and handing off those calls. It's just... it's not good.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself being easily startled or having an exaggerated reaction to sudden noises or movements?\n\nPatient: Um, startled? Yeah, oh my gosh, yes. It's actually getting kind of embarrassing. Like, every single time a door slams, or the printer jams, or even if someone just comes up behind my desk, I jump like something terrible is happening. My heart just races and it takes me several minutes to actually settle down and breathe normally again. \n\nWhich is especially bad because our office printer is constantly jamming. It's this massive, ancient machine and it always says it's out of cyan ink, even though we only print black and white spreadsheets. It makes zero sense. My coworker, Greg, tried to fix a paper jam in it on Wednesday and ended up getting black toner all over his dress shirt. He looked like a chimney sweep. Have you ever seen Mary Poppins? The original one with Dick Van Dyke? His cockney accent in that movie is so famously bad, but I still love it. I used to watch it on VHS all the time at my grandma's house. She had this entire bookcase filled with those puffy white plastic VHS cases. You know the ones? They always squeaked when you opened them. Her house always smelled like dried lavender and peppermints. She kept those little strawberry candies in a glass dish on her coffee table—the ones with the wrappers that look like actual strawberries? I haven't seen those in a store in years. I wonder if they even make them anymore. I used to eat so many of them I'd get a stomach ache.\n\nBut um... wait, sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, jumping at noises. Yeah. Every time I hear a sudden noise like that printer, I just jump out of my skin and it takes me a while to calm down.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the experience at the warehouse has changed your opinion of yourself or other people in any way?\n\nPatient: Um, changed my opinion? No, I don't think so. I haven't really noticed any change in how I view myself or other people. I mean, I'm still just me, and people are still just people, right? \n\nSpeaking of people, though, my neighbor down the hall just adopted a parrot. An actual, full-sized macaw. It is so loud. I can hear it squawking through the walls when I'm trying to watch TV in the evening. Who even gets a parrot in an apartment building? It feels like one of those pets you should only have if you live on a farm or, like, a pirate ship. I went to a pirate-themed restaurant once when I was a kid on vacation in Florida. They had this whole indoor lagoon where two ships battled while you ate your dinner. I ordered chicken fingers, but they called it \"parrot meat\" on the menu, which completely freaked me out. I refused to eat it, so my dad had to trade me his hamburger. He wasn't very happy about it because it was one of those expensive tourist trap places where a burger costs like twenty dollars. We bought a plastic sword in the gift shop afterward, and I accidentally hit my brother in the eye with it on the car ride back to the hotel. He had to wear an ice pack on his face the whole next day at Disney World. \n\nBut um, yeah, my opinion of myself and others? No, I don't feel like that's changed at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you normally enjoy since the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um, lost interest? No, not really. I don't think so. I still like doing the things I usually do. \n\nLike, I was just thinking about going to my pottery class this weekend. I've been taking this wheel-throwing class for a few months now, and it is so much harder than it looks on TV. You know that scene in Ghost? With Demi Moore and Patrick Swayze? Everyone thinks pottery is going to be like that, all romantic and smooth, but mostly it's just me getting wet clay all over my jeans and accidentally collapsing my bowls because I pushed too hard on the sides. The instructor is this guy named Lars who wears Birkenstocks even in the dead of winter. He always talks about \"centering your energy\" before you center the clay, which sounds a little woo-woo to me, but he makes these gorgeous, massive vases, so I guess it works for him. I tried to make a mug for my mom for Mother's Day, but the handle fell off in the kiln, so now it's just a really lopsided pencil holder. She keeps it on her desk at work, though, which is sweet. She works at a bank, and she has this whole collection of weird pens that look like flowers or animals. She said people are less likely to steal a pen if it has a giant fuzzy flamingo on top of it. I guess that makes sense. \n\nBut um... what was the question? Oh, losing interest in things. No, I still want to go to my class and everything. I haven't noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt less interested in things you used to enjoy since the warehouse inspection?\n\nPatient: Um, less interested? Didn't you... I feel like we just talked about this, right? Like with my pottery class? No, I still like doing all my normal stuff. \n\nActually, I was just telling my friend Sarah about the pottery class. We went to grab coffee yesterday at this new place that opened up down the street from my apartment. It's one of those super trendy cafes where they don't even have a menu on the wall, you just have to scan a QR code on the table. And the tables are made of, like, reclaimed barn wood or something. They had this lavender honey latte that sounded really good, but it was like seven dollars. Seven dollars for coffee! I remember when I was in college, I practically lived at this dingy little diner near campus where you could get a bottomless cup of drip coffee for two bucks. The waitresses there all called everyone \"hon\" and they had these vinyl booths patched up with duct tape. I used to sit there for hours studying for my underwriting exams. I spilled coffee on my textbook once and the pages got all warped and smelled like roasted beans for the rest of the semester. It was the worst. \n\nBut yeah, no, I haven't lost interest in anything. I'm still doing all my usual hobbies."
